# 2026-10-09: tube apex cap, curve changes, and the range-of-motion goal (both lines)

Run files: `claude_diag/runs/<run>/<run>.feb` (results in the .xplt beside it); notes in `runs/run_log_notes.csv`.
Ba / Bp / C as Bump 1996 (tools/pop_q_bump.py; mm, + below the hymen). "Motion" = end - rest along the hymenal normal
(tools/rom_report.py).

## 1. Tube apex cap (the user's open task from 2026-10-08/09)

- A rotatable 3D viewer of the corners (claude_diag/apex_view/*.html; tools/apex_view_data.py) showed the hole: a ~5 x 5 mm
  lateral window at each corner between the PVW, the cervix (wrapping over the top) and the curves' end (depth 87 -> 93).
- The approved shape (apex_view/apex_proposal2.html; the user: "I think that's good enough"): the curves' U carried on up the
  canal, every layer wrapped round the cervix / PVW lateral profile at its own depth, narrowing with the cervix to its tip,
  the U's mouth closed over the last 4 layers. tools/apex_contour.py (624 hexes, 0 inverted, 306 node ties). NOT IN SOURCE.

| run | change | t | failed | Ba / Bp / C |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L234_curvepress_apexclosed_pmc_cx3_P1` | old cap, remeshed PM, cervix x3 | 1 | 109 | -15.0 / -16.5 / -49.4 |
| `L241_curvepress_apexcontour_pmc_cx3_P1` | **new cap** instead | 1 | 48 | -14.9 / -16.5 / -48.8 |
| `L241_curvepress_apexcontour_P1` | new cap on the old mesh, cervix x1 | stopped 0.947 | 110 | the cervix (x1) runs away (node 6113, mid-cervix, 22 mm from the cap); at t 0.945 = L202 |

The cap moves the answer <= 0.6 mm; its J stays 0.93-1.28 and its ties keep their rest offsets (median 0.03 mm): the corners
stay closed. The cervix blow-ups are inside the soft cervix, not at the corners: the cervix x3 (not the cap) prevents them.

## 2. The user's curve changes (tube; on L241_curvepress_apexcontour_pmc_cx3_P1)

(1) the curves' AVW half where it lies on the cervix (rings 0-21, depth ~58-86) + the cap -> Vagina_Cervix; (2) the CL / USL /
AVW-Para / Parcus wall ends moved onto the curves' outer surface, one node from the halves' meeting node (CL / Para to the AVW
side, USL / Parcus to the PVW side); the PM-end springs left in place (the user). tools/build_batch242.py, tools/curve_rings.py.

## 3. The new goal: range of motion (the user, 2026-10-09)

"We're no longer trying to match the numbers from the paper ... get about the same range of motion ... isn't too restrictive";
"Range is just that the Bp and Ba can get at least to the points that they got to in the paper. If the paper has apical
location check for that too. Healthy case should stay unprolapsed"; later: "we're not trying to match P1 and P2 anymore. We're
just trying to be able to get those ranges, but maybe with different impairments." Standing settings: remeshed PM, cervix x3,
supports and walls at the source (Load-LA x1/3, sphincter side x0.25, chain mass x10 and ledges x0.1 kept; tube walls x0.35
kept for now). Limits (Luo et al. 2015, 140 cm H2O): Ba >= +10 (Fig. 8A), Bp >= +10 (Fig. 7A), apex motion >= 35 mm (Fig. 6,
0.25 mm / cm H2O), healthy Ba, Bp < 0. Cases: tools/build_batch243.py (ledge), tools/build_batch244.py (tube, from a P1 file);
MAX = levator 60 %, apical 80 %, anterior 50 %, posterior 95 % impaired.

### Ledge line (L228_ext5sl01_pmc_pmd_sph025 + cervix x3)

| healthy support | healthy Ba / Bp | best Ba | best Bp | best apex motion | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| none (source; `L243_ledge_cx3_src_*`) | +14.4 / -15.1 (prolapsed) | +29.1 (P2) | +40.5 (F7A) | 54.4 (F7A) | ranges met, healthy NOT |
| CL x5 (`L246_ledge_cx3_cl5_healthy`, `L247_*`) | -1.2 / -17.5 | +10.0 (P2) | +3.3 (P2) | 27.7 (P2) | too restrictive (stopped) |
| CL x10 (`L246_ledge_cx3_cl10_healthy`) | -4.7 / -17.9 | | | | not followed (x5 already restrictive) |
| paravaginal compression x0.1 (`L250_ledge_cx3_paracomp01_healthy`) | +0.5 / -19.4 (prolapsed) | | | | not enough |
| **paravaginal compression x0.3** (`L246_ledge_cx3_paracomp03_healthy`, `L248_*`, `L251_*`) | **-7.0 / -20.8** | **+38.4 (MAX)** | **+40.1 (MAX)** | **68.1 (MAX)** | **every limit met** |

Paravaginal compression = the AVW-Para springs carry compression at 0.3 x their first slope (tools/build_batch217.py; NOT IN
SOURCE: the source's connectors are tension-only and go slack as the AVW descends, so the AVW has no support and impairing it
changes nothing). With it, impairing the anterior support releases the AVW (F8A Ba +14.2) and the posterior / apical
impairments still reach the posterior and apex limits (F7A Bp +22.6, apex 65 mm; MAX +40.1, 68 mm). The paper's own P1 / P2
then give Bp -7.2 / -3.0 (no longer a target).

## 4. Candidates for the user (not adopted)

- **Ledge:** `L246_ledge_cx3_paracomp03_healthy` = L228_ext5sl01_pmc_pmd_sph025 + cervix x3 + AVW-Para compression x0.3 (one
  departure, NOT IN SOURCE); the impaired cases L248_* / L251_* reach every limit.
- **Tube:** `L258_tubecand_*` (above): three departures from the L242 base (curves x0.6, sphincter x0.25, no curve pressure).
  Its anterior-only impairment does not release the AVW (the closed tube holds it); Ba >= +10 comes from the apical / levator /
  posterior impairments (MAX). The ledge's paravaginal compression does not help the tube (L260_*).
- Decisions for the user: adopt these; whether compression in the paravaginal springs is acceptable (the source's are
  tension-only); whether the pressure on the curves (added 2026-10-06, L189+) should go; the tube walls x0.35 (deferred).

## 5. 2026-10-10: tube main adopted; curve pressure, lofts, the curve-end ballooning; the ledge without compression

The user: "adopt your version as the tube main" (`L258_tubecand_P1`, run_log.py BASES), "run a version with pressure on
distal half of the curves as well as fully on the curves", "do a loft + springs for the tube as well", and for the ledge:
"don't add compression on the springs yet as I don't think that's physiologically accurate. You can try it with the loft +
springs ... See if you can get the healthy ledge to not have a prolapse."

## 6. 2026-10-10 evening (user away): the curve-end tearing runs

Swelling measure: `tools/curve_swell.py RUN ...` (V / V0 of every canal_wrap_hex_* element, 5-tet split, at the last converged
state; a negative ratio = a hex folded inside out). Tube main MAX: max 6.1 (27827-27830, right distal PVW curve), 19 elements
> 2x, swelling from t 0.5; and the LEFT distal elements 27168 / 27169 FOLDED (-0.6 / -0.9): the self-penetration the user saw.

| run (one change on L258_tubecand_MAX) | change | Ba / Bp, apex motion | max V/V0, > 2x, folded | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L258_tubecand_MAX` | the tube main | +18.4 / +17.2, 36.3 | 6.1, 19, 27168 / 27169 | |
| `L276_tubemain_noembed_MAX` | canal_wrap_embed removed | +20.9 / +18.7, 37.3 | 3.4, 20, none | ties make the peak + fold; but the ends slip 15-19 mm off the PVW / PeB: not a fix |
| `L276_tubemain_noembed_healthy` | same, healthy | -12.8 / -15.9 (main -14.0 / -17.5) | 1.9 (main 1.8) | healthy stays up |
| `L276_tubemain_spreadembed_MAX` | 58 curve-end nodes embedded (was 20) | +17.7 / +14.8, 35.6 | 4.0, 16, 27168 / 27169 worse (-1.1 / -1.3) | lowers the peak, not the fold; costs 2.4 mm Bp |
| `L277b_tubemain_selfcontact15_MAX` | self-contact radius 1.5, two_pass 1, + 32 distal faces | | | crawled from the first step, stopped |
| `L277c_tubemain_selfcontact15_nofaces_MAX` | radius 1.5 + two_pass 1 only | | | crawled from the first step, stopped |
| `L277e_tubemain_search10_MAX` | radius 1.0 only | | 4.9x at t 0.009 | stuck at t 0.009, stopped: radius >= 1 mm grabs the curve's own faces at rest |
| `L277d_tubemain_twopass_MAX` | two_pass 1 only | = main at t 0.739 | = main (4.3, 27169 folded) | changes nothing, stopped |

Conclusions: (1) the 20 point ties are what tear the curve end (peak 6x and the fold); without them the range is a little
LARGER, so the range does not depend on them; (2) but without them the curve ends leave the PVW / PeB, and spreading the ties
over more nodes moves the fold instead of removing it; (3) no self-contact setting stops the fold. This supports the proposed
conforming junction (the curves' last ring sharing PVW / PeB nodes, no point embedding): for the user to decide.

## 7. 2026-10-10 night / 10-11: the junction rebuild (the user: "get rid of the flares and make the curves attach better to the PeB")

Picture: `junction_view/junction_before_after_right.png` (+ `_left`): depth slices, tools/junction_slices.py; 3D:
`junction_view/current.html` (tools/junction_view_data.py). What the slices showed: the curve's PVW face shares the PVW's edge
nodes down to depth 5.3 mm; below that the PVW's last layer (build_batch136's flare, |x| 6.0 -> 8.5 at the PVW / PeB face, 108
nodes shared with the PeB) cuts through the curve's last two rings, which were held only by the 20 point ties.

Rebuild (tools/build_batch278.py): the PVW's last layer straight (its own 100 new bottom nodes + 8 curve nodes, tied onto the
PeB top facets; the PeB keeps its width), the curves' face column joined to the straight PVW face (4 merged nodes / side, the
rest facet-tied), canal_wrap_embed removed; `full` also ties 8 curve end nodes onto the PeB top (2 left free: snapping them
would crush a hex). Overnight power cut: the first runs died at t 0.27-0.47 and were restarted.

| run | Ba / Bp, apex motion | curve swelling max, > 2x, folded | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `L258_tubecand_MAX` (main) | +18.4 / +17.2, 36.3 | 6.1, 19, 27168 / 27169 | |
| `L278_junction_MAX` (full) | +17.8 / +15.6, 35.6 | 3.7, 17, 27168 / 27169 still | limits met; peak 40 % lower; the fold stays |
| `L279_junction_nopeb_MAX` (no PeB ties) | +15.3 / +12.8, 34.6 | 6.1, 18 | no better than the main: the PeB ties are what help |
| `L278_junction_healthy` | -14.9 / -19.3 (main -14.0 / -17.5) | 1.8 | healthy met |
| `L279_junction_nopeb_healthy` | -14.2 / -17.9 | 1.8 | healthy met |
| `L278_junction_F8A` | Ba -12.3 (main -11.0) | 1.7 (main 1.95) | Ba not met (as the main) |
| `L278_junction_P1` | -7.5 / -12.6 | | |

Why the fold stays: in MAX the wall everts past the hymen and rolls over its attachment to the PeB: the PVW and the curves above
descend 16-19 mm, the curve end tied to the PeB 13 mm, so the last ring is crushed along the canal (edges to 36 %) and bulges
sideways (to 2.3x). The thin PVW bends through it (J 0.83-1.33); the 2.3 mm curve U with 2-3 coarse rings cannot.
Refinement (tools/build_batch280.py: curve rings split along the canal; NOT IN SOURCE, mesh only):

| run (on L278_junction_MAX) | refinement | Ba / Bp, apex | swelling max, folded | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L280_junction_fine3_MAX` | last 3 PVW-side rings x 3 | +18.0 / +15.7, 35.7 | 3.4, one mild fold higher up (27756, depth 14.7: -0.10) | limits met; end rings no longer fold; ~2x slower |
| `L281_junction_fine44_MAX` | last 4 rings x 4 | | | stopped at t 0.016: steps ~1e-4, impractical |
| `L282_junction_fine2z13_MAX` | every whole ring (AVW + PVW) within 13 mm x 2 | +16.3 / +12.3, 34.9 | 3.2, none (smallest 0.00) | fold-free; Bp -3 mm, apex 0.1 short |
| **`L283_junction_fine2z18_MAX`** | **every whole ring within 18 mm x 2** | **+17.5 / +14.6, 35.5** | **3.3, none (smallest 0.20)** | **limits met, fold-free: the best junction** |

Other cases on the refined (3 x 3) junction: `L280_junction_fine3_healthy` -14.8 / -19.2 (healthy met, = L278);
`L280_junction_fine3_P1` -7.1 / -12.4 (= L278); `L280_junction_fine3_F8A` Ba -12.1 (= L278). `L283_junction_fine2z18_healthy`

**Junction candidate for the user: `L283_junction_fine2z18_*`** = the tube main + no PVW flare (straight last layer tied onto the
PeB) + curves joined to the straight PVW and tied onto the PeB top (no point ties) + every curve ring within 18 mm of the PeB

hex and the swelling roughly halved (3.3x vs 6.1x); ~1.5-2x slower than the main.

## 8. 2026-10-14/15 (user away ~10 h): the curves fold through themselves (the user: L283 MAX "the sides still penetrate themselves around 0.65 on one side and 0.69 on the other")

Measured with tools/curve_selfpen.py (curve surface nodes inside another curve hex; none at rest): in
`L283_junction_fine2z18_MAX` both curves fold through themselves at their distal (PeB) ends, rest z -28..-41, inside the split
rings: 0.36 mm at t 0.65, 0.52 at 0.66, 1.1 mm at 0.68, max 1.36 at 0.8, ~1.0 at t 1. Every earlier junction does the same
(~1 mm from t 0.6-0.7: L258, L278, L280_fine3, L282), so the refinement did not cause it. L283 healthy: none; F8A / P1: <= 0.24 mm.
Cause: the curves' self-contact curve_all_self (ours, NOT IN SOURCE: one-pass PENALTY, penalty 5 auto, search_radius 0.5)
holds the fold until it is 0.5 mm deep, then loses the node. The distal faces start touching at t 0.33 (6 faces; 36 by 0.6).

| run | change (one) | outcome |
|---|---|---|
| `L284_junction_sr10_MAX` | whole curve_all_self search_radius 1.0 (the user's request) | stuck at t 0.0003 |
| `L284_junction_sr075_MAX` / `_pen25_` / `_pen100_` / `_auglag_` | whole self-contact 0.75 / penalty 25 / 100 / AUGLAG | stuck at t 0.004-0.008 |
| `L284_junction_ctrl_MAX` | none (exact copy of L283) | ran as L283 step for step (t 0.73 at step 1221): the crawl is the change |

Where L284 stuck: NOT the fold but the curves' upper end, where canal_wrap_hex_avw_cervix meets apex_contour (rest (+-20, -30,
+31); curve_all holds 24 + 24 apex_contour faces and 2 faces there are in contact from t 0.005 in L283): a wider / stiffer
self-contact pushes the two meshes apart (15 mm by t 0.004). So (tools/build_batch285.py) the 570 curve_all facets with every
node at rest z < -15 moved to their own self-contact curve_distal_self; the upper part keeps L283's contact unchanged.

What folds: free curve nodes (none tied) go into curve hexes that are far away on the mesh (75 of 123 > 8 element hops, median
12 mm apart at rest), all within the distal end (rest z -26..-40), mixed AVW-side / PVW-side / split rings; 81 of 123 cross a
face that IS in the self-contact surface. So the contact sees them and loses them: no penalty / radius / formulation tried
holds the fold; the stiffer ones stall where the distal faces first touch (t 0.33) or against the tied tip.

**Spring contact (the user, 10-17: "a spring be a solid continuum element ... then it can have contact"; shell_tests/gen_dc.py, gen_rope.py):**
FEBio 4.13 has "discrete contact" (spring nodes vs a surface, penalty, closest-point projection without a search radius) and
"discrete contact2" (for deformable-spring "wire" domains), but neither is usable from the .feb file: <constraint type="discrete
contact" surface="S"> parses, yet no attribute or child attaches the springs (discrete_set / discrete / set / ... rejected;
without one it runs and the chain passes straight through the plate); "discrete contact2" fails to initialise, and a <discrete
type="wire"> domain makes FEBio exit while reading. FEBio Studio has no reference to either. A spring as a thin SOLID rope
(0.5 x 0.5 mm hexes, uncoupled mixture: weak Mooney-Rivlin + tension-only fiber-exp-pow along the rope) DOES contact a shell
plate with sliding-elastic: penalty 1 lets the plate cut 0.25-0.45 mm into the rope, penalty 10 holds it face to face.

| L359 wall / cervix vertical motion (mean u_z, mm) | `L352` vs `L359`; then L369 / L368 | **the curve pressure jacks the apex UP, then it drops**: cervix (_PickedSet346) mean u_z L352 -4.4 / -4.2 / -9.2 / -12.7 at t 0.4 / 0.6 / 0.8 / 0.86; L359 +5.0 / +18.4 / +22.9 / +21.1 (22 mm ABOVE rest), then +10.3 (0.889), -2.9 (0.901), -13.1 (0.913), -21.4 (0.927): a 42 mm drop in 0.04 t (the slam), the distal PVW / posterior curve then flies off (PVW min u_z -25 -> -320 mm at 0.927 = the crash). Mechanism: Load-curves_surf normals all point OUT of the curve hexes (512 / 512; Load-AVW 884 out / 0 in, as FEBio's convention), so the pressure squeezes the soft, nearly incompressible curves (Curve_soft x0.6 of the source since L258; k / mu ~84) from outside and they LENGTHEN along their axis: they push the apex up and the PeB end down (why the distal span stays 1.2x and the hairpin is gone); at ~0.88 the long pushed curve column gives way and the stored lift is released in a snap. **Not removed by the milder cases**: L369 (P-arcus x0.075) cervix +17.5 / +21.3 / +18.2 / +12.5 (0.6 / 0.8 / 0.88 / 0.899, starting to drop); L368 (la60) +18.4 / +23.0 (0.6 / 0.8) |

Force from the table at the median elongation (tools/spring_stretch.py --fam USL): **L381 (x0.2)**: elongation top / bottom 29.0 / 38.7 mm (t 0.6), 34.0 / 44.9 (0.85) -> per side 0.64 / 1.07 N (both sides 1.3 / 2.1 N), tangent 0.08-0.09 N/mm per side; **L386 (x2)**: 6.4 / 18.2 (0.6), 11.4 / 25.4 mm (0.85) -> per side 0.83 / 1.40 N (both 1.7 / 2.8 N), tangent 0.10-0.18 N/mm. **The force is about the same (~1-1.4 N a side); x10 only reaches it after 11-25 mm instead of 34-45 mm**: the source law has a 25 mm nearly slack toe (0.009 N/mm top, 0.0009 bottom), so at x0.2 the cervix travels forward through the toe before the USL bite. Nothing in the model is very stiff: the cervix's forward motion is set by a small net force (~1-1.5 N a side, the wall pressures largely cancel) against very soft supports, so a few tenths of a newton move it ~17 mm. The lift is the curve jack (half pressure in L386): blocked forward, the lengthening curves push the cervix up instead |
